### Post-10.10 development — October 2026 (shipped in 10.11)
- [x] **CI built with the wrong compiler, which hung `test_settingsdialog` and put the release installer at risk**: the 10.11 release CI stalled indefinitely at suite 7 (`settingsdialog`) on every commit that contained it, while it passed locally in under a second. **Diagnosis**, through a throwaway `feature/` branch: a first guess (`QPrinterInfo` blocking without a print spooler) was stubbed and disproved. Running the exe directly on the runner showed it never started: exit `0xC0000139` `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND`. Under ctest that loader failure shows as a modal "Entry Point Not Found" dialog that nobody can close, hence the hang. The import table explained it: `install-qt-action` installs the Qt 6.4.3 `win64_mingw` DLLs, which are built with MinGW 11.2 (MSVCRT), but not the toolchain itself. CMake therefore picked the runner's preinstalled `C:\mingw64`, a newer UCRT GCC, and those binaries load Qt's older bundled `libstdc++`. Most suites happened to use only symbols present in both; `settingsdialog.obj` pulled one that is not. The release job ships an exe built the same way, so this was a latent risk for the published installer, not just a test problem. **Fix**: Qt's own `tools_mingw1120` is no longer on the Qt online repo, so both CI jobs install MinGW GCC 11.2.0 posix-seh MSVCRT from Chocolatey (`mingw 11.2.0.07112021`, winlibs) and pass it to CMake explicitly. The release job also puts it on `PATH`, so `windeployqt` deploys the matching runtime. A new `Check runtime of built executables` step fails the build if any exe imports the UCRT `api-ms-win-crt-*` DLLs. The printer stub was reverted because it was not the cause. Kept as hardening: a 120 s CTest `TIMEOUT` on every suite (CI also passes `--timeout 120`) and `timeout-minutes: 45` on the build job, so a future hang fails instead of holding a runner for the 6 h default. Files: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tests/CMakeLists.txt`, docs.
- [x] **opencode configuration removed**: the project no longer uses opencode, so `opencode.json` (its command wrappers pointing at the skills) and `.opencode/agents/dead-code-finder.md` (a copy of the Claude subagent) were deleted. A sweep found no other live reference; the remaining mentions in this tracker are historical entries. Claude Code's `.claude/skills/` and `.claude/agents/` are the single source for AI-agent workflows.
- [x] **Configuración → Verifactu: show/hide toggle for the service key**: the ServiceKey field was masked with no way to reveal it, so the user could not check what had been typed or pasted. A checkable **Mostrar / Ocultar** `QToolButton` now sits beside the field and switches the `QLineEdit` echo mode between `Password` and `Normal`. It changes the display only: the text, the save path and the DPAPI-at-rest storage are unchanged. A button was chosen over an eye-icon `QAction` because the project ships no eye icon and a text-only trailing action would render blank. Hidden by default comes free: both callers (`MainWindow`, and the first-run prompt in `main.cpp`) build the dialog on the stack for each open. New suite **`test_settingsdialog`** (15 suites now) drives the real dialog offscreen, pointing `AppSettings` at a throwaway file through the existing `loadFrom` seam and never accepting the dialog, so the real `~/.laideal_settings.json` is untouched. It checks masked by default, reveal without changing the text, hide again, and masked on reopen. Verified non-vacuous by stubbing out the toggle's `setEchoMode`. Files: `src/appsettings/settingsdialog.cpp`, `tests/{test_settingsdialog.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, docs.
- [x] **Contabilidad reports end with a detail annex of the tickets and gastos behind each summary**: requested so the user can audit or debug exactly what each figure is made of. Every period (trimestral, mensual, and each quarter of the annual report, placed after the consolidated summary) now ends with **Detalle de ingresos**: one line per paid ticket with nº recibo, fecha de pago, cliente, garment count, base, IVA and importe. It is followed by **Detalle de gastos**: fecha, nº factura, empresa, servicio, IVA %, base, cuota and importe. Each table ends with a total row. **Reconciliation by construction**: the issue warned against duplicating the `WHERE`, so the period predicates were lifted into shared constants in `sql_lite.cpp` (`kIngresosIncomeWhere`, `kGastosPeriodWhere`). The existing `totalPriceBetweenDates` / `countOperationsBetweenDates` and the new `incomeTicketsBetweenDates` / `expensesBetweenDates` all use them, as does the grouped `annualAccountingByQuarter`, which previously kept its own copy. A detail table therefore cannot list a row its summary excluded. `totalPriceBetweenDates` now accumulates and returns `double` instead of `float`, so a large quarter's total row cannot differ by a cent from the summary. The `verifactu-compliance-auditor` review confirmed the shared predicate is identical to the previous inline SQL and flagged both of these points. **Release-review follow-ups (10.11)**: a gastos row with a NULL or non-standard IVA (e.g. 4 %) was listed and totalled in the detail but summed in no summary column. Such rows are now marked `*`, kept out of the detail total, and explained in a note under the table. `ExpenseDetail::iva` is `-1` for NULL, so NULL is not mistaken for sin IVA. The annual annex no longer runs 8 per-quarter scans: `sql_lite::annualDetailsByQuarter` fetches the year once per table and buckets rows by quarter through the same collectors as the period listings. It is asserted equal to the four per-quarter calls, including a ticket paid across two quarters. The reconciliation tests now hold the `double` totals in `double`. All three were verified non-vacuous. Ingresos lines aggregate garment rows by `n_recibo`, so the line count equals "Número de tickets". The annual summary still comes from the grouped `annualAccountingByQuarter`, which `test_annualAccountingByQuarter` already pins to the per-period helpers. Rendering is in the pure statics `Contabilidad::createHtmlDetailIngresos` / `createHtmlDetailGastos`, with HTML-escaped client and company names and borderless zebra tables for multi-page output. `contabilidad` now links `sql_lite` PUBLIC because its header exposes the row types. Tests: `test_sql_lite` asserts both listings reconcile with the total and count helpers, including ANULADA, RECTIFICADA, voided-in-place and half-open-end rows. `test_contabilidad` covers the renderers. Both were verified non-vacuous by breaking the listing filter and the gastos base split. The PDF layout itself is not machine-checked; review a generated report in the app. Files: `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/contabilidad/{contabilidad.h,contabilidad.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, `tests/{test_sql_lite.cpp,test_contabilidad.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, docs.
- [x] **Recogida de Prendas `Importe total` no longer counts voided garments**: reported from the shop. A search by Pago date for 07-08-2026 added ticket 31045 to the total although it had been voided. **Root cause**: `voidGarmentRow` (local void via Anular prendas) keeps `pagado = 'NO'` but stamps `fecha_pago` with the void date, so a voided garment matches a Pago-date search, and the total loop in `on_pb_search_clicked` summed `importe` over every proxy row. The first fix followed the issue text and counted only paid rows. The 10.11 release review then caught that this applies to every search mode: an unpaid ticket searched by number showed 0.00 instead of the amount owed, and a Recepción search lost the day's intake. The final rule, chosen by the user, skips only rows excluded from every total, via the pure `sql_lite::garmentExcludedFromTotals`: `verifactu_estado` `ANULADA` (voided in place or cancelled at AEAT) or `RECTIFICADA` (superseded). Unpaid rows count again. The excluded states now live in one list, `kTotalsExcludedEstados`, which builds both this C++ check and the `kIngresosIncomeWhere` SQL, so the panel and Contabilidad cannot drift apart. The listing still shows voided rows, which is useful for auditing. Test `test_garmentExcludedFromTotals` was verified non-vacuous. Files: `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/recog_prendas/recog_prendas.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **New AI components adapted from addyosmani/agent-skills, gaps only**: reviewed the 4 agents and 25 skills there and adopted only what this mature repo lacked. Generic duplicates were skipped: `code-reviewer`, `security-auditor` and `code-simplification` are covered by the built-in `/code-review`, `/security-review` and `/simplify`; planning, docs and git-workflow skills are covered by `/tackle-issue`, `/update-docs` and `/release`; the web-only ones (Core Web Vitals, browser DevTools, frontend UI) don't apply. Each new piece is rewritten for Qt/C++/CTest and points at existing docs rather than copying them. **`test-engineer` subagent** (adapted from theirs): finds the coverage gap and writes Qt Test cases using the four seam patterns from `architecture.md` §Testing. It must prove every new test fails against the bug, which is the repo's recurring "verified non-vacuous" practice, and it reports a needed seam extraction instead of refactoring production code on its own. It preloads `/coding-guidelines`. **`/debugging` skill** (adapted from `debugging-and-error-recovery`): reproduce from the shop report, `~/.laideal.log`, a **copy** of the live DB (never the DB itself) or the captured AEAT response; localize by layer with a symptom table drawn from past fixes; reduce, fix the cause, guard, verify. **`verifactu-compliance-auditor` subagent** (project-specific, read-only, proactive): checks a diff against the ten requirements in `verifactu-requirements.md`, the one review a generic reviewer cannot do. **`guidelines-auditor` subagent**: runs the existing `coding_guidelines_audit.md` method and returns tiered findings plus a delta, mirroring how `dead-code-finder` feeds its report. `/tackle-issue` now routes bugs through `/debugging`, may delegate coverage to `test-engineer`, and runs the compliance auditor on Verifactu-sensitive diffs; the existing steps are unchanged. Files: `.claude/agents/{test-engineer,verifactu-compliance-auditor,guidelines-auditor}.md`, `.claude/skills/{debugging,tackle-issue,update-skills}/SKILL.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/{ai_agent_instructions,INDEX,coding_guidelines_audit}.md`.
- [x] **AI-agent layout migrated from `.claude/commands/` to `.claude/skills/`**: the five workflows (`coding-guidelines`, `release`, `tackle-issue`, `update-docs`, `update-skills`) moved (`git mv`, history kept) to `.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, the layout Claude Code now documents as standard (commands still work but are the legacy form). The real gain is the new YAML frontmatter, not the move. Legacy commands were already offered to the model, but with no `description` the model only saw the H1 title ("Code Development Guidelines"), so auto-triggering was weak. Each skill now has a trigger-first `description`. **Invocation control**: `/release` sets `disable-model-invocation: true` (it merges to `master`, pushes a tag and publishes, so it must never fire on its own); `/coding-guidelines` sets `paths: src/**, tests/**, **/CMakeLists.txt` so it auto-activates only when code is touched; the rest stay user + auto. `/tackle-issue` and `/release` gained an `argument-hint`. `/update-skills` was rewritten for the new format: skill vs subagent, frontmatter fields, invocation flags, and agent frontmatter, with links to the official docs. Also fixed `/tackle-issue`'s self-contradicting "200-char cap" (the rule is 300). `dead-code-finder` was already in the correct `.claude/agents/` layout and is unchanged. References updated in `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ai_agent_instructions.md` (plus `/review` → `/code-review`), `docs/INDEX.md` (plus a broken relative agent link), `docs/coding_guidelines_audit.md` and `opencode.json`. Adopting more agents and skills (e.g. adapting addyosmani/agent-skills' `code-reviewer`, `test-engineer`, `security-auditor` to Qt/C++) is left as a separate follow-up. Files: `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `opencode.json`, docs.

### Post-10.9 development — September 2026 (shipped in 10.10)
- [x] **AEAT reconciliation picks the accepted record, not the first one (ticket 31121)**: reported from the shop — "Actualizar con los datos de AEAT" stayed greyed out on an invoice that was demonstrably registered (the operator validated it by hand in the AEAT sede). Root cause found in a captured log: **the vendor stores every submission ATTEMPT, not one record per invoice.** The query for `31121` returned `Count: 5` — four duplicate-rejections (`ErrorCode 9999`, `CSV: null`, `ValidationUrl: null`, one per retry the operator had made) plus the original acceptance from 08-09 carrying `StatusResponse "Correcto"`, `CSV "A-…"` and the AEAT `ValidationUrl`. The accepted record was the **last** element, and the parser took `Items[0]`, so it read a failure: no CSV → `hasUsableCsv()` false → the adopt button disabled. Fixed by scanning for the accepted record (CSV present, no `ErrorCode`, not `IsRejected`) and falling back to the first only so the dialog can still show what came back; `VerifactuRemoteRecord` gains `recordCount`, and the dialog now explains when several attempts were returned and distinguishes "datos no coinciden" from "ningún registro fue aceptado". Note the earlier single-record fixture could not have caught this — it took a real retried ticket to expose it. Regression test built from the captured 5-record shape (redacted), verified non-vacuous by restoring `Items[0]` and watching it fail. Files: `src/verifactu/{verifactutypes.h,verifacturesponse.cpp}`, `src/recog_prendas/recog_prendas.cpp`, `tests/test_verifactu_response.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Reprinted recibos now show IMPORTE PAGADO for a paid ticket**: reported from the shop after 10.9. The 10.9 fix covered `PayDialog`'s AEAT-timeout fallback, but the **reprint** path from Recogida de Prendas is different code and hardcoded the marker off at three `buildTicket(..., /*addPayedInfo=*/false)` call sites in `Imprimir`, so a fully-paid ticket reprinted from the pickup panel never showed the payment. Fixed for the two recibo sites; the third (the multi-event loop) and the single-seq factura branch stay `false` deliberately — a factura implies payment and omits the marker on every path, including `MainWindow::printFra`. The rule is **all garments paid, not any**: a reprinted recibo shows the whole ticket total, so "any paid" would mark a partially-paid ticket as settled in full. Extracted as the tested seam `sql_lite::ticketAllGarmentsPaid()` (strict `pagado='SI'`, so a blank never reads as paid, plus a `COUNT > 0` guard so an empty result cannot print the marker on nothing) rather than reading `Imprimir`'s live model inline — which also keeps it out of the integration-only bucket its sibling `checkAnyItemPaid()` sits in. Verified non-vacuous by restoring the hardcoded `false` and watching the behaviour revert. Files: `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/imprimir/imprimir.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Reception date made display-only in Recogida de Prendas**: the sibling of the payment-date fix shipped in 10.9. `de_date_recep` had no `dateChanged`/`editingFinished` handler either, so edits to it were silently discarded for the clicked row — but unlike the payment date it was not inert: its one remaining reader is `SEPARATE_GARM`, which copies it onto the split-off row. So typing a date changed nothing visible, then silently applied to a garment split off afterwards. Now `setReadOnly(true)` with no spin buttons and a tooltip, which is also the correct semantic (a split-off garment belongs to the same ticket and arrived the same day) and closes a subtler hazard: `fecha_recepcion` is what the Verifactu startup-recovery floor gates on (`sql_lite::pendingVerifactuEvents`), so a hand-typed value could shift a row in or out of the recovery window. Pure UI wiring, not unit-testable; the DB-level consequences are already covered (`insertGarmentRow` persists what it is handed, `pendingVerifactuEvents` pins the floor behaviour). Files: `src/recog_prendas/recog_prendas.cpp`, `docs/modules/recog_prendas.md`.
- [x] **Progress tracker archived down to a readable active section**: the file had grown to 419 lines against a 250 soft limit. Everything from the pre-8.0 `feature/add_mdiago_verifactu` build-out downwards was moved below the Archive header (nothing deleted, per the tracker rules), cutting Completed Milestones from 343 to 126 lines. Because that block already sat immediately above the Archive header, the move is a two-line diff — the header simply relocated above it.

### Post-10.8 development — September 2026 (shipped in 10.9)
- [x] **Manual smoke test formalised as `docs/smoke_test.md`, and the three bugs it caught**: the automated suite proves the pure and DB-level logic but structurally cannot reach the real network, the real printer, the migration running on a real database, or Qt signal/slot wiring with no testable seam. A full manual pass against a copy of the live DB (19 501 rows) found three defects, all fixed: **(1)** `PendingSubmitsDialog` persisted a literal `'Error'` instead of the canonical `ERROR`; `verifactuEstadoFromString()` compared exactly, so those rows decoded as *un-submitted* and silently lost their "Reintentar envío a AEAT" button (it tests `== VerifactuEstado::Error`), and the QR-blocking check in `Imprimir` was defeated too. Fixed at the writer (bind `verifactuEstadoToString`), normalised for existing rows in `migrateDatabase` via `UPPER()`, and the reader is now case-insensitive as a safety net — the stored value had to be corrected because the SQL filters elsewhere compare case-sensitively. **(2)** "Consultar en AEAT" was gated to unsettled rows only, which blocked the one way to verify what AEAT holds for a registered invoice; it is now offered on any **paid** row (the query is read-only) while the adopt button is disabled where there is nothing to adopt. **(3)** The Recogida de Prendas payment-date field was **inert** — nothing ever wrote it back, its only writer being the unreachable `PAY_YES` path — so operator edits were silently discarded; made display-only, since `fecha_pago` is part of the AEAT invoice identity and editing it after submission would make a retry register a second invoice and stop reconciliation matching. The checklist is now a release gate (`/release` step 1). Files: `docs/smoke_test.md` (new), `src/app/pendingsubmitsdialog.cpp`, `src/sql_lite/sql_lite.cpp`, `src/verifactu/verifactutypes.h`, `src/recog_prendas/recog_prendas.{h,cpp}`, `tests/{test_verifactu_models,test_sql_lite}.cpp`.
- [x] **Migration verified to move zero money, on real data**: income per quarter compared against a genuine pre-migration `backups/` snapshot came out identical to the cent (2026 Q1 8 470.24, Q2 14 777.65, all-time 219 745.98), while `PENDIENTE 349 → 30` and `SIN COBRAR 0 → 319` — 349 = 30 + 319, and `ANULADA`/`ENVIADA`/`ERROR`/the 18 678 legacy NULL rows all unchanged. This is the empirical backing for scoping the backfill to the literal `'PENDIENTE'`: had blanks been normalised too, that would have been an 18 678-row rewrite altering what prints on invoices. Note a copy of the live DB taken *after* the new build has run is **not** a valid baseline (it is already migrated) — the `backups/` snapshots are.
- [x] **`GetFilteredList` confirmed working against the live service, and the parser pinned to the real schema**: a captured reply proved the endpoint answers, the envelope is **`Items`**, and — decisively — that the `InvoiceID` filter **is applied server-side** (`Count: 1` for a specific id, not the whole table). Every first-choice field name guessed while blind turned out correct (`InvoiceID`, `InvoiceDate` as ISO-8601 with a time component, `TotalAmount`, `CSV`, `StatusResponse`, `ValidationUrl`). The speculative fallbacks were then **removed**: once the shape is known they are a liability, because the same record also carries `ExternKey` and `Created`, which are *not* the InvoiceID and *not* the invoice date, and a wrong-field match is worse than no match. The real payload also revealed `IsRejected` and `ErrorCode`, now required to be clear before a CSV may be adopted — a record AEAT rejected can still come back from a query. Fixtures captured from live traffic are committed **redacted** (NIF, company name, user e-mail, CSV) because the repository is public. Files: `src/verifactu/{verifactutypes.h,verifacturesponse.cpp}`, `tests/test_verifactu_response.cpp`.
- [x] **Reconcile an "already exists" rejection against AEAT's own record (`GetFilteredList`)**: the remaining half of the offline/slow-AEAT problem. Once AEAT has registered an invoice whose reply we lost, no retry can ever succeed — it can only answer "ya existe" — so the row was stuck at ERROR with the operator's only recourse being the AEAT sede by hand. **This supersedes the assessment recorded in the 8.x "operator-surfaced recovery dialog" milestone below**, which rejected auto-reconcile on the grounds that the Irene Solutions Kivu API had "no confirmed idempotent lookup-by-InvoiceID endpoint". That turned out to be wrong: the vendor's own REST client exposes **`<base>/GetFilteredList`** (confirmed against `mdiago/VeriFactu`'s `Net/Rest/ApiClient.cs` + `Config/Settings.cs`, same base URL we already use), and our `rest_api.md` had documented the matching `FilterSet`/`Filter` objects all along — naming `InvoiceID` as a valid `FieldName` — under "Not implemented (reserved for future)".
  **What is unknown, and how it is contained**: the vendor does not publish the *response* schema (its own client parses it into an untyped `ExpandoObject`) and there is no sample response anywhere reachable. Since flipping a row to ENVIADA asserts a regulatory fact, the design never trusts the parse alone: `parseVerifactuQueryResponse()` probes several plausible envelopes (`Return`/`Records`/`List`/bare array) and field spellings, and crucially **distinguishes "AEAT does not have it" from "we could not read the answer"** — a failed query or an unparseable body yields `parsed=false`, never a confident absence. `verifactuRemoteMatches()` then requires the InvoiceID, the date **and** the amount to agree to the cent, treating a missing date or a zero amount as missing evidence rather than agreement. Only then, and only with a non-empty CSV, is the write offered — and it is always the operator who confirms it, in a dialog showing AEAT's record beside ours with the raw payload one click away. The raw JSON is also always logged, so the first real reply is what lets the candidate field names be narrowed to the truth.
  **The write** is `sql_lite::reconcileVerifactuFromAeat(db, nRecibo, seq, csv, url)`: sets ENVIADA + CSV + validation URL + timestamp, clears the error, scoped to the event's **paid** rows, and refuses an empty CSV or a row already `ENVIADA`/`ANULADA`/`RECTIFICADA` (re-stamping those would revive a deliberately superseded invoice). **Triggers**: a "Consultar en AEAT" button in the Verifactu detail dialog for any unsettled row, and an automatic query when a submission is rejected as duplicate — detected by `verifactuErrorIsDuplicate()`, matched on wording (Spanish + English stems) because no stable code is published. Read-only against AEAT: `queryInvoiceAsync` answers on its own `queryFinished` signal so it can never be mistaken for a submission reply.
  Tests: `test_verifactu_response` gains the parser (envelope + alternative spellings + ISO dates + comma decimals + nested detail), the absent-vs-unreadable distinction, the duplicate-wording detector and the full match gate; `test_sql_lite` gains the reconcile write and its four refusals. The match gate was verified non-vacuous by weakening it and watching the test go red. **Caveat: the parser fixtures are hypothetical shapes, not captured traffic** — they pin the parser's behaviour, not the real schema, which stays unconfirmed until the first live query. The dialog wiring itself is untested UI. 14 suites green. Files: `src/verifactu/{verifactutypes.h,verifacturesponse.h,verifacturesponse.cpp,verifactumanager.h,verifactumanager.cpp,verifactuintegration.h,verifactuintegration.cpp}`, `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/recog_prendas/{recog_prendas.h,recog_prendas.cpp}`, `tests/{test_verifactu_response,test_sql_lite}.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Offline / slow-AEAT handling: four defects behind the "ticket sin Pagado ni QR, y al día siguiente dice que ya existe" field report**: traced from the customer report. Root trigger is that **both UI waits are shorter than the network layer's own timeout** — `VerifactuManager` sets a 10 s transfer timeout while MainWindow waits 3 s and PayDialog 5 s — so a merely *slow* AEAT (not just a dropped line) routinely leaves the UI having given up on a request that is still perfectly alive. Four independent fixes, each committed separately:
  1. **The QR-less recibo now says IMPORTE PAGADO.** `PayDialog::printPartialFactura` hardcoded `addPayedInfo=false`, but it is only ever reached *after* `persistPayment` marked the rows `pagado='SI'` — so on an AEAT timeout the customer got a ticket showing neither the payment nor a QR (the QR is absent by design: `isRecibo = qrCode.isNull()`, and recibos never carry one). Now passes `ui_impr->isRecibo`, i.e. the marker appears exactly on the recibo fallback; facturas keep omitting it as every other print path does. New `test_escpos::test_renderPaidMarker` pins the renderer contract both ways.
  2. **A transport failure is no longer recorded as a definitive ERROR.** PayDialog already treated `NETWORK_ERROR`/`PENDING` as an *unknown* outcome, but MainWindow and the RecogPrendas retry passed any non-success straight through to `Error`. New pure `verifactuEstadoForResult(status)` (SUCCESS→Enviada, ERROR→Error, everything else→NotSubmitted) is now used inside `updateTicketVerifactuFields`, fixing every caller at once. An unknown outcome also **keeps its InvoiceID** so a retry reuses the identity AEAT may already hold. This was the main engine of the report: an `ERROR` row offers a "Reintentar" that, when AEAT *did* register the invoice, can only ever answer "already exists".
  3. **The RecogPrendas retry is seq-aware.** It submitted the **bare `n_recibo`** with `SUM(importe)` over the **whole ticket** on the **reception** date — so retrying a partial payment sent the wrong amount under an InvoiceID belonging to a different event, on a date AEAT never keyed it on (a third route to "already exists", and one that could have registered a wrong amount if it did *not* collide). New tested seam `sql_lite::verifactuEventFor(db, nRecibo, seq)` returns that event's own paid total and original `fecha_pago`; `retryVerifactuSubmit(ticketNum, seq)` uses it and refuses to submit when no paid event exists. `RecogPrendas::m_pendingSubmits` becomes `reqId → {ticketNum, seq}` so the reply patches the right rows.
  4. **A late AEAT reply is no longer discarded.** `PayDialog` is stack-allocated by `RecogPrendas` and dies with `exec()`, and its timeout cleared `m_pendingReqId` — so a reply arriving at, say, 7 s hit `requestId != m_pendingReqId` and was dropped, **including a SUCCESS carrying the CSV**. That guaranteed a falsely-`PENDIENTE` row whose next-day recovery retry was doomed. The dialog now emits `submitAdopted(reqId, ticketNum, seq)` before closing and `RecogPrendas` adopts the request, so the existing handler applies the reply. On a late success both PayDialog and MainWindow now tell the operator the **factura with QR can be printed** rather than silently reporting a CSV for a ticket the customer already left with.
  **Wait policy deliberately unchanged** (3 s / 5 s): with (4) in place a longer wait buys only the chance of printing the QR on the first pass, at the cost of freezing the counter for up to 10 s on every sale when AEAT is slow. The late-confirmation message covers the same need without the hang. Raising the waits to match the 10 s transport timeout remains an option if the shop would rather wait than reprint.
  **Not unit-tested**: (4) is Qt signal/slot + dialog-lifetime wiring, and (1)'s call-site wiring; a harness would need a fake `VerifactuIntegration` that emits `requestFinished` on demand plus headless-constructible `RecogPrendas`/`PayDialog`. The DB-level consequences of both are covered (`updateTicketVerifactuFields` tests). 14 suites green. Files: `src/verifactu/verifactutypes.h`, `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/recog_prendas/{pay_dialog.h,pay_dialog.cpp,recog_prendas.h,recog_prendas.cpp}`, `src/app/{mainwindow.h,mainwindow.cpp}`, `tests/{test_escpos,test_verifactu_models,test_sql_lite}.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **A ticket's first partial payment no longer stamps the unpaid remainder with the AEAT result**: found while auditing the `SIN COBRAR` split for regressions (the split itself was clean; this turned out to be a pre-existing bug going back to the 8.5 partial-payment feature). `nextVerifactuInvoiceSeq` computes `COALESCE(MAX(verifactu_invoice_seq), -1) + 1` **over rows where `pagado = 'SI'`**, so for a ticket with no paid rows yet that set is empty and the first payment event gets **seq 0** — which the still-unpaid siblings also carry (already pinned by `test_nextVerifactuInvoiceSeq`). `PayDialog::persistPayment` correctly scopes the payment itself to the selected hashes, but the AEAT write-backs then ran `WHERE n_recibo = ? AND verifactu_invoice_seq = ?`, which also matched the unpaid remainder: those garments were stamped `ENVIADA` + CSV + an InvoiceID for an invoice that never covered them (or `ERROR`/`PENDIENTE` on a failure/timeout). Quarterly income was never affected — every accounting query gates on `pagado = 'SI'` first — but the mislabelled rows became **non-voidable in "Anular prendas"** (`garmentIsLocallyVoidable` requires an unsubmitted estado) and fed a spurious CSV into the print path's `hasCsv` check. Fixed by adding `AND pagado = 'SI'` to both write-backs (`sql_lite::updateTicketVerifactuFields` and `PayDialog::markPendingVerifactu`). That predicate was chosen over scoping to the paid hashes because it also covers the two **retry** paths (`RecogPrendas::retryVerifactuSubmit` and the `PendingSubmitsDialog` recovery retry) which share the same seq-0 exposure but have no hash list; it is correct for all four callers, since an AEAT result can only ever belong to a paid row. `nextVerifactuInvoiceSeq` was deliberately left alone — starting the first event at seq 1 would change the AEAT InvoiceID of a ticket's first payment from the bare `<n_recibo>` to `<n_recibo>-1`, a submission-identity change with no upside once the write-backs are scoped. `updateTicketVerifactuFields` had **no test coverage at all**, which is how this survived; two now pin it (success and AEAT-error), both verified to fail without the guard. `test_sql_lite` gains an include path to `src/verifactu` (header-only `VerifactuResult`, no lib link). 14 suites green. Files: `src/sql_lite/sql_lite.cpp`, `src/recog_prendas/pay_dialog.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, `tests/CMakeLists.txt`, docs.
- [x] **`VerifactuEstado` split into `Unpaid` ("SIN COBRAR") and `NotSubmitted` ("PENDIENTE")**: closes the Open Non-Blocking entry of the same name, the residual from issue #43. `verifactu_estado` now says which of the two situations a row is in instead of leaving it to be inferred from `pagado`/`fecha_pago`: **SIN COBRAR** = unpaid, no invoice to send; **PENDIENTE** = paid and due at AEAT (submitted and awaiting a reply, or not yet sent). Writers pick per row — `MainWindow::saveTicket` and `AddGarment::saveFactura` both branch on `row.pagado`; `PayDialog` and the rectificativa placeholder keep writing PENDIENTE because both are paid by construction. **The load-bearing risk is on the read side**: paying a garment does *not* rewrite `verifactu_estado`, so a row is still SIN COBRAR at the exact moment `RecogPrendas` decides whether to submit it — a gate testing `== NotSubmitted` alone would have silently stopped paid garments from reaching AEAT (a compliance failure far worse than the cosmetic bug #43). Both such gates now go through the new pure helper `verifactuEstadoIsUnsubmitted()` (true for Unpaid, NotSubmitted, and legacy blank): `sql_lite::garmentIsLocallyVoidable` (keeps "Anular prendas" working on the new state) and the `RecogPrendas` PAY_YES submit trigger. Other read sites needed no change — they compare against `ENVIADA` exactly, or block only on `ANULADA`/`RECTIFICADA`/`ERROR`, and the accounting filters (`!= 'ANULADA' AND != 'RECTIFICADA'`) pass SIN COBRAR through exactly as they did PENDIENTE. **Migration**: a one-time idempotent backfill in `migrateDatabase` re-labels unpaid `'PENDIENTE'` rows as `'SIN COBRAR'`. It is scoped to the *literal* `'PENDIENTE'` and deliberately leaves NULL/`''` alone — those are legacy split-off garments, and `Imprimir`'s event-list query plus `CancelInvoiceDialog` detect them via `verifactu_estado != ''`, so making them non-empty would have added a spurious event group to printed invoices. `pendingVerifactuEvents` keeps its #43 payment gate as belt-and-braces (the estado filter now excludes SIN COBRAR on its own, but the gate still covers those untouched legacy blanks). Tests: `test_verifactu_models::test_estadoIsUnsubmitted` pins the "every other gate treats the two alike" contract, `test_estadoRoundTrip` covers the new value, `test_sql_lite::test_migrateDatabase_backfillsUnpaidAsSinCobrar` pins both migration boundaries (a paid pending row survives; a blank row stays blank) plus idempotency, `test_pendingVerifactuEvents_excludesSinCobrar` and an extra `garmentIsLocallyVoidable` case cover the readers. 14 suites green. Files: `src/verifactu/verifactutypes.h`, `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/app/mainwindow.cpp`, `src/add_garment/add_garment.cpp`, `src/recog_prendas/recog_prendas.cpp`, `src/app/voidgarmentsdialog.h`, `tests/{test_verifactu_models,test_sql_lite}.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **CI: bump Node 20 actions to v5**: GitHub Actions was force-running `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/upload-artifact@v4` and `actions/download-artifact@v4` on Node 24 and warning that Node 20 is deprecated; the shim will eventually be removed and break the build + release pipeline. A first pass to `@v5` only half-fixed it — CI still warned, because **`upload-artifact@v5` and `download-artifact@v5`/`v6` are themselves Node 20**; only `checkout@v5`, `upload-artifact@v6+` and `download-artifact@v7+` run on Node 24 (verified by reading `runs.using` in each major's `action.yml`). Final state: `checkout@v5`, `upload-artifact@v7`, `download-artifact@v8` in `ci.yml` and `checkout@v5` in `docs.yml`. Upload/download were taken to the current matched pair — v7 and v8 are the two halves of the same direct-upload feature — and the three inputs actually used (`name`, `path`, `if-no-files-found`) are unchanged. `setup-python@v5` / `upload-pages-artifact@v3` / `deploy-pages@v4` were left alone: not flagged, and out of scope for a warning fix. **Verification gap**: `download-artifact` runs only in the tag-gated `release` job, so a `develop` push exercises the upload side only. If v8 ever misbehaved the release job would fail at the download step, which happens *before* packaging and publishing, so a broken artifact cannot reach the GitHub release. Files: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/docs.yml`.

### Post-10.7 development — September 2026 (shipped in 10.8)
- [x] **"Envíos Verifactu pendientes" no longer lists unpaid tickets (issue #43)**: reported from the shop — on startup the recovery dialog showed *every* ticket at `verifactu_estado='PENDIENTE'`, none of them paid, so none of them ever due for AEAT submission. Traced the save path: `MainWindow::saveTicket` stamps **every** garment row `PENDIENTE` (`VerifactuEstado::NotSubmitted`) regardless of `pagado`, while the actual submission is gated on `if (isPaid)` in `on_bb_save_reset_clicked` — an unpaid ticket is correctly never sent, but its rows sit at `PENDIENTE` until collection and payment in `PayDialog`. The dialog's feed, `sql_lite::pendingVerifactuEvents`, filtered only on estado + the reception-date floor, with **no payment gate**, so the normal steady state of the shop (garments in store awaiting collection) read as unreconciled in-flight AEAT submissions. The string `"PENDIENTE"` conflates "not due to be sent" with "sent, reply lost"; only `onRetryClicked` distinguished them, one step too late (it refuses a row with no `fecha_pago`). **Why now**: the default recovery floor is `2026-09-01`, so from 1 September every newly-entered ticket cleared the floor and the dialog began filling up. Fixed by adding `AND pagado = 'SI' AND fecha_pago IS NOT NULL AND fecha_pago != ''` to the query — `fecha_pago` is the real gate (it is what a retry must re-submit under), `pagado` is the weaker literal-string check kept alongside it. Verified no recoverable event is lost: every writer that leaves a genuinely pending row sets both columns — save-time paid ticket (`fecha_pago = fecha_recepcion`), `PayDialog` partial pay (`seq>0`), and the `RectifyInvoiceDialog` placeholder (`pagado='SI'`). New `test_sql_lite::test_pendingVerifactuEvents_excludesUnpaid` mirrors the corner case both ways (same `n_recibo`: unpaid save-time event excluded, paid partial-pay event still surfaced) plus a `pagado='SI'` row with no `fecha_pago`; confirmed it fails without the gate. 14 suites green. Files: `src/sql_lite/sql_lite.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.

### Post-10.6 development — July 2026 (shipped in 10.7)
- [x] **Anular prendas: stamp the cancellation date, and keep observations editable on a voided row**: two in-shop follow-ups to the "Anular prendas" tool. (1) `sql_lite::voidGarmentRow` now also writes `fecha_pago` **and** `fecha_recogida` with the current date (`dd-MM-yyyy`), so the DB records *when* a garment was anulada — previously both stayed empty and the cancellation had no timestamp anywhere. Accounting is unaffected: the row keeps `pagado='NO'` and gets `verifactu_estado='ANULADA'`, and every accounting query filters on `pagado='SI'` plus a not-`ANULADA` estado, so a stamped `fecha_pago` can never leak into a quarter total. (2) In **Recogida de Prendas**, the `observaciones` field is no longer read-only for an Anulado row: `updateRowClickedToFields()` leaves `le_obsv` writable and `updateDb()`'s Anulado early-return now exempts the `OBSV` op, so staff can record why the garment was voided or add later notes. All other edit paths (pay / pickup / split / re-price) stay locked. Tests updated: `test_voidGarmentRow_setsAnuladoAndScopesByHash` asserts both dates equal today, and `test_markTicketPickedUp_excludesAnulado` now asserts a "Recoger todo" does not overwrite the voided row's cancellation date. 14 suites green. Files: `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/recog_prendas/recog_prendas.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
  - **Hardening prompted by the above (accounting-lock read made order-independent)**: because a voided garment now carries `edit_lock 0` **and** a stamped `fecha_pago`, it becomes a matching row in the month-lock lookup `sql_lite::readLockForMonthAndYear` (which keys on `fecha_pago LIKE '%-MM-yyyy'`). That function previously took the **first** matching row's `edit_lock`, so a voided (unlocked) row sorting ahead of a genuinely locked sibling could make an already-locked month read as **open** and let a write through. Changed the query to `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(edit_lock), 0)` so the month reads locked if **any** row is locked, regardless of order; an empty month still returns 0 (callers only branch on `== 1` / `== 0`, and `Facturas` relies on empty → 0 to allow an expense in a fresh month). `readLockForQuarter` already used the `COUNT(*), COALESCE(MAX(edit_lock),0)` shape, so this only aligns the month variant. `test_readLockForMonthAndYear` extended with an unlocked-row-inserted-first case that returns 1 (would have returned 0 under the old read). Files: `src/sql_lite/sql_lite.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.

### Post-10.5 development — July 2026 (shipped in 10.6)
- [x] **Status API watchdog recalibration (warning was suppressed on lid-open)**: after the shop reinstalled APD6 (which fixed the `BiOpenMonPrinter` hang), the diagnostic showed every call fast, but lid-open prints still showed no warning. Cause: the watchdog timeout (4 s) was **below** the offline `BiDirectIOEx` send time, so a normal offline send tripped the watchdog first -> it returned `status.valid=false`, suppressed the warning, and latched the API off for the session. Key finding from the field logs: the DLL's `ERR_ACCESS` (`-80`) path on an open cover / no paper **returns after its own internal ~5 s and ignores our `BiDirectIOEx` timeout parameter** (lowering it 5000 -> 2500 ms did not speed up the `-80` case). So the fix is to size the **watchdog well above** that: set it to **10 s** (was 4 s, briefly 6 s which left only ~0.3 s margin over the real ~5.5 s offline send and would still occasionally false-trip). A genuine hang is indefinite, so 10 s cleanly separates "normal offline" from "hung". Confirmed on the shop TM-T20III: lid-open now shows the warning and queues via RAW, normal prints are unaffected. Root cause of the whole saga (`BiOpenMonPrinter` hang) was a printer **firmware update** that reset the interface config; reinstalling the APD6 Status API package restored it. Files: `src/printing/statusapiprinter.cpp`, `src/imprimir/imprimir.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Status API diagnostic button ("Probar Status API") to pinpoint the firmware-induced hang**: to find *which* `Bi*` call the printer firmware update made block, added `StatusApiPrinter::runDiagnosticsBounded(queue, timeoutMs)` - it opens the printer and calls each Status API function in sequence (`BiOpenMonPrinter` -> `BiGetStatus` -> `BiGetType` -> `BiDirectIOEx` a real-time `DLE EOT 1` status request that does not print -> `BiCloseMonPrinter`), logging `[diag]` a line before and after each call with elapsed ms, and returns a report. It runs behind the same watchdog thread, so a hang cannot freeze the UI: the culprit shows as the last `->` line with no result after it, and the report ends with a "BLOQUEO" note. A **"Probar Status API (diagnóstico)..."** button in SettingsDialog (General tab, beside the Status API checkbox) runs it and shows the report (also in the log). `src/appsettings` now links `printing` for `StatusApiPrinter`; the `DLE EOT` request is safe (real-time status, no paper used). Not unit-tested (Win32 DLL / UI). 14 suites green. Next: run it on the shop TM-T20III and read which call stalls. Files: `src/printing/{statusapiprinter.h,statusapiprinter.cpp}`, `src/appsettings/{settingsdialog.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, docs.
- [x] **Status API watchdog: a hung Epson DLL can no longer freeze the app**: after a **printer firmware update** on the shop TM-T20III, enabling the Status API froze the app on every print - one of the synchronous `Bi*` DLL calls (`BiOpenMonPrinter`/`BiGetStatus`/`BiDirectIOEx`) now blocks indefinitely on that printer, and it ran on the UI thread. Since the DLL is third-party and out of our control, wrapped it in a watchdog: new `StatusApiPrinter::sendAndReadStatusBounded(..., timeoutMs)` runs the DLL calls on a **detached worker thread** and waits at most 4 s (`std::thread` + `condition_variable`; not `std::async`, whose future destructor would re-block). On timeout the UI thread abandons the worker (its `shared_ptr` result slot keeps it memory-safe), falls back to RAW, and **latches the API off for the rest of the process** so only one print pays the 4 s, not every one. `Imprimir::printTicket` now calls the bounded variant. Net effect: the optional Status API can never freeze the POS again - worst case is one 4 s delay then silent RAW. Not unit-tested (threading/DLL/UI wiring, no pure logic); 14 suites still green. Workaround already in the field: the shop turned the setting off to unblock. Files: `src/printing/{statusapiprinter.h,statusapiprinter.cpp}`, `src/imprimir/imprimir.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Status API cover-open detection: read-before-send + `isFatal` split + send-failure warning (hardware-driven)**: iterated on the shop TM-T20III to make the optional Epson Status API actually warn on an open cover. (1) Reordered `StatusApiPrinter::sendAndReadStatus` to the APD6 manual's flow - `BiOpenMonPrinter` -> **`BiGetStatus` (read first)** -> `BiDirectIOEx` (send) - since the send returns `ERR_ACCESS` (-80) while the cover is open, so a send-first design never read status. (2) New `PrinterStatus::isFatal()` splits faults: **fatal** (cutter/mechanical/unrecoverable) -> warn and do NOT print; **recoverable** (cover/paper) -> warn but fall through to RAW so the spooler queues the job. (3) **Hardware finding**: this unit reports an open cover as `ASB_NO_RESPONSE` (raw ASB `0x1`), not `ASB_COVER_OPEN` (0x20) - `BiGetStatus` succeeds but decodes to "ready", and the send fails -80. So a specific "Tapa abierta" isn't obtainable here; `Imprimir::printTicket` now warns generically ("comprueba tapa / papel / conexión") whenever the API send is refused **and** we reached a valid status, and still queues via RAW. A missing DLL leaves status invalid -> stays silent (RAW only). Kept a gated `qDebug` of the raw ASB word in `StatusApiPrinter` (only logs when nonzero / read error) to identify the offline cause in the field. New `test_escpos::test_printerStatus_fatalVsRecoverable` (pure decode/classification). The `printTicket` warning wiring is UI-coupled, not unit-tested. 14 suites green. **Validated on the shop TM-T20III (APD6 Status API installed)**: the warning dialog appears for lid-open, no-paper and printer-off, and the ticket still queues via RAW and prints once the printer is fixed. Hardware quirk recorded: this unit reports printer-off as raw ASB `0x1` (`ASB_NO_RESPONSE`) and both lid-open and no-paper as `0x4` (and the value is not even stable per condition), so a specific "Tapa abierta" / "Sin papel" is not obtainable here - the send-failure-keyed generic warning is the right design. This closes the former "Validate the ESC/POS Status API on hardware" open issue. Files: `src/printing/{printerstatus.h,printerstatus.cpp,statusapiprinter.cpp}`, `src/imprimir/imprimir.cpp`, `tests/test_escpos.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Test coverage: Imprimir InvoiceID selection extracted to a pure seam**: the "which InvoiceID to print" rule in `Imprimir::displayInvoiceId()` was a first-non-empty scan over the ticket rows' `verifactu_invoice_id` column with a bare-`n_recibo` fallback, trapped behind `Imprimir`'s `QSqlQueryModel`/`QLineEdit` members and so untested. Extracted the pure decision into `sql_lite::verifactuDisplayInvoiceId(const QStringList &invoiceIds, const QString &fallback)` (sibling of the existing `verifactuInvoiceId` format seam); `displayInvoiceId()` now just gathers the column and delegates. New `test_sql_lite::test_verifactuDisplayInvoiceId` locks the invariant that matters - it picks the first NON-EMPTY literal (not row 0, which can be an unpaid row of a multi-event ticket with an empty id) and falls back to the bare `n_recibo` for all-empty legacy (8.0-8.4) rows. The remaining `getTicketInfo` seq `WHERE` filter and `resolveQrCode` QR gating stay parked as integration-only (see Backlog). 14 suites green. Files: `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/imprimir/imprimir.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **RecogPrendas: integer-only quantity (follow-up to the pre-payment editing feature)**: the review of the 10.5 feature noted that `le_qty` accepted a decimal like `2,5`, which `toInt()` would store as cantidad `2` while the importe recompute used `2.5` - a divergence. Fixed at the source: `initialSettings()` installs a `QIntValidator(1, 9999)` on `le_qty` so a non-integer count can't be typed, and Qt suppresses `editingFinished` while the field is not `Acceptable`, so the existing `newQty < 1` guard in the `QTY` case is now belt-and-suspenders. Files: `src/recog_prendas/recog_prendas.cpp`, docs.

### Post-10.2 development — July 2026
- [x] **RecogPrendas: edit quantity / service / importe before payment**: the garment-pickup panel now lets staff correct a garment's quantity, service and amount while it is still unpaid, instead of only observations/size. `le_qty` and `le_price` became editable and the read-only `le_servic` line edit was replaced by a `cb_servic` `QComboBox` offering the two services the app prices (`Limp.` / `Plan.`). Editing quantity (`QTY` op) or changing the service (`SERVICE` op) re-prices the row automatically via `sql_lite::garmentImporte` (`importe = qty * unitPrice * size`, unit price from `readGarmentPrice`); `le_price` (`PRICE` op) is a manual comma-normalised override applied last. All four widgets are gated by `priceEditable = !isAnulado && !isPaid && !editLock` in `updateRowClickedToFields` (a paid row was already submitted to AEAT), and every `updateDb` case re-checks `!editLock && pagado=="NO"` as defense-in-depth. The combo writes on `activated(int)` (user interaction only) so loading a row never fires a spurious write, and a legacy `servicio` not in the list maps to `findText()==-1` -> blank rather than a wrong value. New DB seam `sql_lite::updateGarmentServiceAndImporte` (servicio + importe) with `test_sql_lite::test_updateGarmentServiceAndImporte`; QTY reuses the existing `updateGarmentQtyAndImporte` seam and PRICE reuses `updateTicketSizeAndPrice`. Files: `src/recog_prendas/{recog_prendas.ui,recog_prendas.h,recog_prendas.cpp}`, `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Auto-update fixed for a hardened-`%TEMP%` machine: per-user install + non-elevated launch with `TMP`/`TEMP` redirect**: a shop report that the in-app update "only works when the app is run as administrator" turned into a three-layer investigation on the actual machine. **(Layer 1)** The installer targeted `C:\Program Files\La Ideal` (admin), so it was manifested `requireAdministrator`; `QProcess::startDetached`/`CreateProcess` cannot elevate, so launching it from a non-elevated app failed with `ERROR_ELEVATION_REQUIRED`. A first attempt (**released as 10.3, then retracted**) switched the launch to `ShellExecuteW("runas")` to raise the UAC prompt. **(Layer 2)** On-machine testing showed 10.3 still failed - the downloaded installer in `%TEMP%` could not be executed *at all* (not by the app, not by Explorer: "Windows cannot access the specified device, path, or file... permissions"). Its inherited `%TEMP%` ACL was `Administrators:(F)` + `Everyone:(R,W,DC)` with **no execute for the normal user** - a hardened Temp (the "AV/Controlled-Folder blocks executing from %TEMP%" the README warns about). Under UAC the Administrators SID is deny-only, so a non-elevated process fell to `Everyone` = no execute; the same file in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\<app>\updates` gets `<user>:(F)` (execute allowed). **(Layer 3)** Even after downloading to that executable folder, Inno Setup's own loader extracts its bootstrap into `%TEMP%` and runs it there, so an *elevated* installer hit "Unable to execute file in the temporary directory. Error 5". A direct test proved **UAC elevation resets the environment** (an elevated child saw the default hardened `%TEMP%`, not a `TMP`/`TEMP` we had set) - so redirecting Inno's temp only works if the installer runs **non-elevated**. **Final fix (10.4):** (a) `releases/laideal.iss` sets `PrivilegesRequired=lowest` -> per-user install to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\La Ideal`, no elevation, no UAC; (b) `Updater::downloadInstaller` saves to `QStandardPaths::AppLocalDataLocation + "/updates"` (execute-allowed) instead of `%TEMP%`, clearing stale installers; (c) `UpdaterDialog::onDownloadFinished` sets `TMP`/`TEMP` to that folder via `qputenv` and launches with `QProcess::startDetached` (non-elevated, so the redirect is inherited) - the 10.3 `ShellExecute("runas")` and `shell32` link were reverted. **Validated on the hardened machine**: a minimal `PrivilegesRequired=lowest` Inno installer, run non-elevated with `TMP`/`TEMP` redirected, installed per-user with exit code 0 and no Error 5. **Note:** every build up to 10.3 has the broken (Temp/admin) updater, so 10.4 must be installed **manually once**; auto-update works from 10.4 onward. The old `C:\Program Files\La Ideal` install should be uninstalled once (data is safe - the DB path lives in settings). **Not unit-tested**: environment-dependent download path + process launch + installer packaging, no pure logic; `compareVersions` stays covered. Files: `releases/laideal.iss`, `src/updater/{updater.cpp,updaterdialog.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, docs.

### Post-10.1 development — June 2026
- [x] **Dev-experience: self-healing VSCode Debug + Rectify INSERT clarity comment**: (1) the F5 build task (`.vscode/tasks.json`) now `dependsOn` "CMake: configure (Debug)", so a fresh clone or a wiped `build-debug/` self-configures instead of failing with no exe - CMake no-ops an already-valid cache, so it costs nothing on repeat runs; the README Debug section was trimmed accordingly (the manual one-time-configure step is no longer needed, troubleshooting kept). (2) Added a one-line comment at the `RectifyInvoiceDialog` `INSERT INTO ingresos` explaining it deliberately bypasses `sql_lite::insertGarmentRow` because a rectificativa row writes the full `verifactu_*` column set the `IngresoGarmentRow` seam does not carry - so a future reader does not "fix" it into the seam. Files: `.vscode/tasks.json`, `README.md`, `src/app/rectifyinvoicedialog.cpp`.
- [x] **Global UI language setting (Español/English) via a bundled Qt translator**: replaces per-widget Spanish button hacks with one global `QTranslator`, so every Qt standard dialog (the Yes/No -> Sí/No confirmations, Aceptar/Cancelar, QColorDialog, etc.) follows a single configurable language. New `AppSettings::language()`/`setLanguage()` (`app.language`, default "es"; persisted in `applyDefaults`) + a **Idioma** combo in SettingsDialog -> General (note: applies on next launch). `main.cpp` installs the translator at startup: English is Qt's source language (no translator), Spanish loads `qtbase_es`. The `.qm` is **bundled as a Qt resource** (`resources/i18n/qtbase_es.qm`, prefix `/i18n`, loaded via `:/i18n/qtbase_es.qm`) because the release runs windeployqt with `--no-translations` - Qt's translations dir is absent on the shop PC, so shipping it in the exe is the only reliable path. As required for the global setting to actually take effect, removed the one existing per-widget override: `VoidGarmentsDialog` reverted from manually-built "Sí"/"No" buttons back to a standard `QMessageBox::question(Yes|No)` that the translator localizes. (Domain toggle text like `pb_payment` "SI"/"NO" and `pb_estado` "Recogido"/"En tienda" is **data**, not language, and was left untouched; bespoke buttons such as PayDialog "Cobrar"/"Cancelar" are app strings, not Qt standard buttons, so the translator does not touch them.) Not unit-tested: startup/UI wiring; the language getter/default is covered indirectly by the AppSettings suite pattern. Files: `src/appsettings/{appsettings.h,appsettings.cpp,settingsdialog.h,settingsdialog.cpp}`, `src/app/{main.cpp,voidgarmentsdialog.cpp}`, `resources/{laideal.qrc,i18n/qtbase_es.qm}`, `README.md`, docs.
- [x] **AddGarment refuses to append garments to a paid ticket (follow-up to #41)**: closes the latent "paid-but-unsubmitted" gap - `AddGarment::saveFactura` never submits to AEAT, so a garment added to an *already-paid* (already-submitted) ticket and marked paid would sit paid-but-unsubmitted. Rather than wire a submit into that path, adding is now blocked at search time for any ticket that already has a paid garment: new pure-ish seam `sql_lite::ticketHasPaidGarment(db, nRecibo)` (EXISTS a `pagado='SI'` row); `AddGarment::on_pb_search_pressed` shows "el recibo ya está pagado (enviado a la AEAT); usa un recibo nuevo" and leaves `ticketFound=false` so save is blocked. Only unpaid (not-yet-submitted) receipts can be altered locally by appending garments - consistent with the VoidGarmentsDialog rule for removing them. New `test_sql_lite::test_ticketHasPaidGarment` (unpaid ticket -> false, any paid row -> true, unknown ticket -> false). Files: `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `src/add_garment/add_garment.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Log analysis + message refinement (issue #42)**: analysed a customer log (~43k lines, 75 sessions, May 27 - Jul 1) for misleading traces and warnings. Two code fixes plus a findings triage. **(1) `MainWindow::saveTicket` garment count**: the header logged `rows: <table_ticket->rowCount()>` (always 9, the fixed number of empty input slots) which read as "9 garments saved" even when only 1-2 were filled; the per-row line logged the raw table slot index. Now it counts garments actually inserted (`savedGarments`), logs `saved garment <k>` sequentially per insert and a `- N garment(s) saved` summary at the end. Also corrected the stale comment claiming rows may be written "empty if Verifactu not configured" (saveTicket always writes PENDIENTE). **(2) `BackupManager::performBackup` Qt warning**: `QSqlDatabasePrivate::removeDatabase: connection 'laideal_backup_verify' is still in use` fired on **every** backup (21x in the log) because `removeDatabase()` was called while the block's `QSqlDatabase`/`QSqlQuery` were still in scope. Restructured Step 2 (verify) - and Step 1 (snapshot) for consistency, it had the same latent bug on its error path - to do the DB work in an inner block that captures a success flag, then `removeDatabase()` after the handles go out of scope (the pattern Step 1's success path already used). **Findings triaged as non-actionable** (documented for the record): the ~50 `Verifactu submit failed`/`network error`/`timeout` warnings are legitimate AEAT rejections of *data*, not code bugs (pre-2024 InvoiceDate on a bulk historical re-submission, duplicate-InvoiceID "ya existe" dedup rejections, an HTTP 429 rate-limit, connection timeouts); the single `QProcess: Destroyed while process ("cscript") is still running` is a **legacy** artifact from the pre-10.0 Excel/`.vbs` print pipeline that was removed in 10.0 (current code only uses `QProcess::startDetached` for the updater); and `readMaxNMinYearInColumnFromTable - database path not configured` (2x at startup) is the intended `dbNotConfigured` guard when a report screen is opened before the DB is configured. **Not unit-tested**: both fixes are logging / Qt-connection-scoping with no pure logic to assert, and a `performBackup` happy-path test is impractical because its success path calls `AppSettings::instance()->save()` (writes the real `~/.laideal_settings.json`) - a future test would need an AppSettings injection seam. 14 suites still green. Files: `src/app/mainwindow.cpp`, `src/backup/backup_manager.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **AddGarment now books added garments PENDIENTE (issue #41)**: diagnosed from a customer log (`saveTicket: ticket "30877"` followed by `AddGarment::saveFactura: INSERT INTO ingresos ticket= "30877"` for a "Manta"). Root cause: `AddGarment::saveFactura` ("Herramientas -> Añadir nuevas prendas") built its **own inline INSERT that omitted the `verifactu_estado` column**, so a garment added to an existing ticket was written with NULL/empty estado while `MainWindow::saveTicket` rows get `PENDIENTE` — the estado column read blank for those garments. Fixed by routing the insert through the existing tested `sql_lite::insertGarmentRow(IngresoGarmentRow)` seam (same one `saveTicket` and the SEPARATE_GARM split use) with `verifactuEstado = verifactuEstadoToString(VerifactuEstado::NotSubmitted)` ("PENDIENTE"), which also dedups the duplicated INSERT SQL. **Safe by construction:** the pending-submit recovery query (`sql_lite::pendingVerifactuEvents`) already treats empty/NULL and `PENDIENTE` identically (`verifactu_estado IS NULL OR = '' OR = 'PENDIENTE'`) and refuses to retry rows with no `fecha_pago`, so stamping PENDIENTE changes only the displayed/consistent estado, not the AEAT submission behaviour (no new duplicate-InvoiceID risk). New `test_sql_lite::test_insertGarmentRow_addGarmentShapePending` anchors the regression (an added, unpaid garment persists PENDIENTE). The AddGarment slot itself stays UI-coupled; the meaningful contract (insertGarmentRow persists the estado it is handed) is unit-tested. 14 suites green. Files: `src/add_garment/add_garment.cpp`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Void unpaid, not-delivered garments (issue #40) — new VoidGarmentsDialog + Anulado estado + green-pagado colouring**: a local counterpart to `CancelInvoiceDialog` for erroneous receipts / change-of-mind on garments not yet delivered. The key insight that shaped the design: AEAT submission only happens when a ticket is **paid** (save-if-paid in `MainWindow::on_bb_save_reset_clicked`, or at payment via PayDialog) — an unpaid garment stays `verifactu_estado = PENDIENTE` and was **never sent to AEAT**, so voiding it is a pure local DB change with **no AEAT anulación** (that path is `CancelInvoiceDialog`, for paid/ENVIADA rows). New menu action **Herramientas → Anular prendas (recibo erróneo)...** opens `src/app/voidgarmentsdialog.{h,cpp}`: search a ticket by number, see its garments in a table with per-garment checkboxes, and void the selected ones. Only rows that pass the new pure guard `sql_lite::garmentIsLocallyVoidable(pagado, verifactuEstado)` (unpaid **and** never-submitted) are selectable; paid/ENVIADA rows show "Pagado/enviado - usar Anular Factura" and already-voided rows show "Anulado". Voiding runs the new DB seam `sql_lite::voidGarmentRow(db, nRecibo, hash)` = `UPDATE ingresos SET estado='Anulado', verifactu_estado='ANULADA'` scoped by `(n_recibo, hash)` (`pagado` deliberately left as `NO` — the row is closed, not collected). The new `estado` value lives as `INGRESOS_ESTADO_ANULADO` in `ingresos_schema.h`. For the colouring requirement, `TextColorDelegate` gained a pure static `classify(cellText, rowEstado)` and now reads the row's sibling `estado` column: a row whose estado is `Anulado` renders **green everywhere** (via `INGRESOS_COL_ESTADO`), so a voided receipt's `NO` pagado no longer reads as an outstanding debt — satisfying "cambiar color del pagado a verde aunque siga siendo que NO". Accounting is unaffected: an anulado row is unpaid (`pagado='NO'`) so it was already excluded from the `pagado='SI'` paid totals, and `verifactu_estado='ANULADA'` keeps it out of the Verifactu-based figures. New tests: `test_sql_lite` covers `garmentIsLocallyVoidable` (paid/sent variants) and `voidGarmentRow` (sets Anulado/ANULADA, leaves pagado, scopes by hash); new suite `tests/test_textcolordelegate.cpp` covers `classify` incl. the Anulado-green rule. 14 suites green. Files: `src/app/voidgarmentsdialog.{h,cpp}` (new), `src/app/{mainwindow.ui,mainwindow.h,mainwindow.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp,ingresos_schema.h}`, `src/tableview/textcolordelegate.{h,cpp}`, `tests/{test_sql_lite.cpp,test_textcolordelegate.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, docs.
  - **Follow-up: an Anulado row is now immutable and excluded from payment, prints and accounting everywhere.** After the initial dialog landed, in-shop testing found the RecogPrendas `pb_state` button could still flip a voided garment back to "En tienda". Fixed by treating an Anulado row as read-only across every edit path: (1) `RecogPrendas::updateRowClickedToFields` disables `pb_state` / `pb_pay_all` / `pb_pku_all` / `pb_separ_garm` and makes `le_obsv` / `le_size` / `le_price` read-only for an Anulado row (and the redundant re-enable block in `selectSourceRow` was removed so `updateRowClickedToFields` is the single source of truth); (2) `RecogPrendas::updateDb` early-returns on an Anulado row as defense-in-depth so no write path can revive it; (3) **Recoger todo** now goes through the new seam `sql_lite::markTicketPickedUp(db, nRecibo, fechaRecogida)` whose `UPDATE ... SET estado='Recogido'` carries `AND estado != 'Anulado'`, so a bulk pickup never revives a voided garment; (4) **PayDialog::loadTicket** skips Anulado rows so a voided (unpaid) garment can never enter the payable set and be submitted to AEAT; (5) **Imprimir::getTicketInfo** excludes `estado='Anulado'` on both the recibo and factura queries so a voided garment never prints. **Contabilidad needs no change**: every accounting query already requires `pagado='SI'` (an Anulado row is `pagado='NO'`) and also excludes `verifactu_estado='ANULADA'` — doubly excluded — and the payment guards guarantee an Anulado row can never become `pagado='SI'`. New `test_sql_lite::test_markTicketPickedUp_excludesAnulado` seeds a voided + a normal row and asserts the bulk pickup marks only the normal one Recogido. Files: `src/recog_prendas/{recog_prendas.cpp,pay_dialog.cpp}`, `src/imprimir/imprimir.cpp`, `src/sql_lite/{sql_lite.h,sql_lite.cpp}`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Documentation site (MkDocs Material → GitHub Pages)**: the `docs/` tree is now published as a searchable static site. New `mkdocs.yml` (Material theme, light/dark palette toggle, search, the full `docs/` tree as nav) renders the existing Markdown as-is; a `validation:` block classifies the many cross-repo links (to `src/` sources and root files like `releases_notes.txt`, `.claude/…`) as **warnings, not errors**, so a plain non-strict `mkdocs build` stays green. A minimal root `pyproject.toml` hosts the build tooling as a `docs` optional-dependency group (`pip install ".[docs]"` → `mkdocs-material`); it ships no Python package (`py-modules = []`). New `.github/workflows/docs.yml` uses the official Pages flow (`upload-pages-artifact` + `deploy-pages`, `pages: write` + `id-token: write`, `concurrency: pages` non-cancelling): the **build** job runs on push and PR (the PR run is the pre-merge publication test), the **deploy** job runs only on master push and then **verifies publication** by polling the deployed `page_url` until HTTP 200. Requires the repo's Pages source set to "GitHub Actions" (done). `.gitignore` ignores `site/` / `_site/`. Verified locally: build exits 0 with the expected cross-repo link warnings. Files: `mkdocs.yml`, `pyproject.toml`, `.github/workflows/docs.yml`, `.gitignore`, docs.

### Post-10.0 development — June 2026
- [x] **Thermal ticket layout polish from shop feedback (Uds. gap, condiciones pitch, QR spacing)**: three readability tweaks to the printed recibo/factura after the 10.0 in-shop printouts. (1) The garment table's quantity column abutted the name column (`Uds.Prendas` with no gap because the 4-char header filled the 4-col field) — `EscPosBuilder::garmentRow` default `qtyWidth` widened 4→6 so `Uds.` (and short quantities) clear the `Prendas` column; the name column drops from 35→33 cols @80mm, still ample. (2) The "Condiciones generales" legal block was already the smallest built-in font (Font B) but still read like body text, so its line **pitch** was tightened via two new pure builder methods `lineSpacing(int dots)` (`ESC 3 n`, set to 24) / `defaultLineSpacing()` (`ESC 2`, restored after the block) — the glyphs can't shrink below Font B, but the denser pitch makes it read as fine print. (3) The Verifactu QR had too much whitespace above it — the extra `feed(1)` before the raster was removed (the payment marker above and the AEAT pixmap's own quiet zone already separate it). New `test_escpos` case asserts the `ESC 3 24` / `ESC 2` bytes and the widened qty column; the `test_ticket_preview` ESC-interpreter learned to skip `ESC 2` / `ESC 3 n` (the latter is 3 bytes — the old 2-byte default skip would have leaked the pitch byte into the text); committed `preview_*.png` regenerated. 13 suites green. Files: `src/printing/{escposbuilder,ticketrenderer}.{h,cpp}`, `tests/{test_escpos,test_ticket_preview}.cpp`, `docs/modules/printer/preview_*.png`, docs.

### Post-9.3 development — June 2026
- [x] **Optional ESC/POS Status API layer (dossier Phase 4) — paper-out / cover-open / cutter status**: behind the new `print.use_status_api` setting (off by default), `Imprimir::printTicket` routes the *same* ESC/POS bytes through Epson's Status API instead of the RAW spooler and reads the device status back. Two new `src/printing/` seams: **`PrinterStatus`** (pure — `fromAsb(quint32)` decodes the Epson ASB 32-bit status word into named flags `paperEnd`/`paperNearEnd`/`coverOpen`/`cutterError`/`mechanicalError`/`unrecoverableError`/`offline`/`drawerOpen`, with `hasError()`/`hasWarning()` and a Spanish `summary()`), and **`StatusApiPrinter::sendAndReadStatus`** (Win32 — loads `EPSStmApi.dll` **dynamically** via `LoadLibrary`/`GetProcAddress` so there is no build-time Epson-SDK dependency, then `BiOpenMonPrinter(2,queue)` → `BiDirectIOEx` send → `BiGetStatus` → `BiCloseMonPrinter`; a stub off Windows). The transport returns false **without sending** when the DLL/API is unavailable or the queue can't be opened, so `printTicket` falls back to the RAW `ThermalPrinter::send` path — enabling the flag never stops printing. On a real problem after a successful send it shows a `QMessageBox::warning(status.summary())` ("Sin papel", "Tapa abierta", "Papel casi agotado", …). Settings getter `AppSettings::useStatusApi()` + a SettingsDialog → General checkbox. **Not hardware-validated**: the `ASB_*` bit values and the `EPSStmApi.dll` call path must be confirmed on the shop's TM-T20III with the APD/Status API installed (like Phase 0 for the RAW path). Six `PrinterStatus` decode cases added to `tests/test_escpos.cpp` (OK/near-end/end/cover/cutter/offline + error-ranks-before-warning + default-invalid); 13 suites green. Files: `src/printing/{printerstatus,statusapiprinter}.{h,cpp}` + `CMakeLists.txt`, `src/imprimir/imprimir.cpp`, `src/appsettings/appsettings.{h,cpp}` + `settingsdialog.{h,cpp}`, `tests/test_escpos.cpp`, docs.

- [x] **Excel ticket printing replaced by direct ESC/POS over the Windows RAW spooler**: receipts/facturas no longer go through QXlsx → `.xlsx` → a generated `.vbs` → `cscript` → Excel COM (which required Excel installed and licensed on the shop PC, launched a process per print, was brittle — a left-over `EXCEL.EXE`/dialog/macro prompt could hang the 60 s wait — and gave no print/error feedback). New `src/printing/` static lib with three seams from the research dossier ([`docs/modules/printer/`](modules/printer/README.md)): **`EscPosBuilder`** (pure: chainable ESC/POS byte builder + a PC858 transcode for Spanish accents/`€` + dots/char-width column math), **`TicketRenderer`** (pure: a plain `TicketData` → the same header/identity/garment-table/totals/QR/policy/timestamp content and ordering as before, laid out to the printer's real column width; the factura IVA split reproduces the legacy rounding — IVA derived from the rounded base string — so base+IVA still reconstruct the total), and **`ThermalPrinter`** (the only host-specific piece: `OpenPrinter`/`StartDocPrinter` datatype `RAW`/`WritePrinter` via `winspool`, default-printer fallback, a human-readable `GetLastError`/`FormatMessage` string surfaced in a `QMessageBox`; a failing stub off Windows keeps the builder/renderer cross-platform). `Imprimir::createTicketExcel`→`buildTicket` (fills `m_ticketBytes`) and `printTicket`→`ThermalPrinter::send`; the public two-step call shape (build copy → print → build next copy → print) is preserved, so all six call sites (mainwindow save + print menu, PayDialog Cobrar, RecogPrendas reprint, the multi-seq chooser) keep working with a one-word rename. The Verifactu QR is **rastered from the exact AEAT pixmap** (`GS v 0`, `qr.scaled(192).toImage()`) rather than re-encoded, so the printed QR stays byte-exact with what AEAT registered; `resolveQrCode()` (async `/GetQrCode`, 5 s wait, estado/CSV gating) is unchanged. New settings `print.printer_name` (empty = Windows default printer) and `print.paper_width_mm` (58/80, default **80** → 576/420 dots) editable in SettingsDialog → General (printer combo from `QPrinterInfo::availablePrinterNames()`); the obsolete `ticketExcelPath`/`ticketPrintScriptPath` helpers were removed. `imprimir` no longer links QXlsx — but the vendored `QXlsx/` tree was **left in place for one release** (decoupled, unused) for an easy rollback before the printer is validated on the shop PC; its removal is a new Open Non-Blocking item. New `tests/test_escpos.cpp` (12th suite, links the light `printing` lib, `QTEST_GUILESS_MAIN`) asserts the exact control bytes (init/align/bold/font/size/cut), the PC858 transcode incl. unmapped→`?`, the column math (576→48/64, 420→35/46), and the recibo/factura render fragments incl. the 121→100+21 IVA split and that a QR is rastered. **Not hardware-validated in CI** (dossier Phase 0): the real printer-queue name, the PC858 code page for accents, and the loaded paper width must be confirmed on the shop's TM-T20III. Files: `src/printing/*` (new lib), `src/imprimir/imprimir.{h,cpp}` + `CMakeLists.txt`, `src/appsettings/appsettings.{h,cpp}` + `settingsdialog.{h,cpp}` + `CMakeLists.txt`, `src/app/mainwindow.cpp`, `src/recog_prendas/{pay_dialog,recog_prendas}.cpp`, root `CMakeLists.txt`, `tests/{test_escpos.cpp,CMakeLists.txt}`, docs (`architecture`, `INDEX`, `README`, `ai_agent_instructions`, `modules/{imprimir,printing}.md`, `modules/printer/`).

### Post-9.2 development — June 2026
- [x] **Annual Contabilidad report: 24 per-quarter scans → 2 grouped queries**: the annual PDF called `computeFigures` for all 4 quarters, each running 4 `totalPriceBetweenDates` + 2 `countOperationsBetweenDates` full-table `substr()` scans (~24 scans). New DB seam `sql_lite::annualAccountingByQuarter(db, year)` runs one grouped query per table over the whole year, bucketed by quarter (`quarter = (month + 2) / 3`): ingresos `SUM(importe)` + `COUNT(DISTINCT n_recibo)` under the exact `pagado='SI'` + ANULADA/RECTIFICADA + half-open date filters; gastos `SUM(importe)` bucketed by quarter×iva (10/21/0) plus a per-quarter row count over every rate (matching the existing gastos count). It returns a `QuarterlyAccountingTotals` (per-quarter raw importes/counts, index 0 = Q1). The IVA base/cuota derivation was extracted from `computeFigures` into the pure static `Contabilidad::figuresFromTotals(...)`, now shared by both the per-period path (trimestral/mensual, unchanged) and the annual path (one prefetched `QuarterlyAccountingTotals`), so the figures are identical. **Verification (the reason this was deferred):** `test_sql_lite::test_annualAccountingByQuarter` seeds a multi-quarter DB and asserts the grouped result equals, quarter by quarter, the per-quarter `totalPriceBetweenDates`/`countOperationsBetweenDates` it replaces — the tax math is provably unchanged. **Caveat:** the grouped path sums `importe` in SQL, so it does not raise the comma-decimal corruption dialog the per-row helpers do; the trimestral/mensual reports still call those helpers and keep that guard (and they are run regularly to lock quarters, so corruption still surfaces). The 4 per-quarter `readLockForQuarter` reads stay (cheap, separate concern). Files: `src/sql_lite/sql_lite.{h,cpp}`, `src/contabilidad/contabilidad.{h,cpp}`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Seq-aware startup recovery of partial-pay (`seq>0`) Verifactu timeouts**: the `PendingSubmitsDialog` startup recovery now reconciles timed-out PayDialog partial-payment events, not only save-time / full-ticket submissions. The pending-events query is extracted into the testable seam `sql_lite::pendingVerifactuEvents(db, floorIso)` — it drops the old `AND verifactu_invoice_seq = 0` filter and groups by `(n_recibo, verifactu_invoice_seq)`, so each event surfaces as its own `{n_recibo, seq, fechaPago, cliente, importe}` row with that event's own `SUM(importe)`. The recovered event re-submits under its `fecha_pago` (the original AEAT submission date — date is part of the invoice identity, so reusing reception date would register a second invoice and defeat the duplicate-InvoiceID guard); the recovery floor still gates on `fecha_recepcion`. Seq is threaded through the async retry path: `PendingSubmitsDialog::Entry` and the `retryRequested` signal gain `seq`; the recovery row shows the `<n>-<seq>` InvoiceID via `verifactuInvoiceId`; `onMarkErrorClicked` now scopes its UPDATE by `seq` so marking one event Error never clobbers a sibling event of the same ticket. `MainWindow::verifactuSubmitInvoice` gains `seq=0` and submits `verifactuInvoiceId(ticketNum, seq)` (so a recovered event re-submits under the same ID it stored); `m_pendingSubmits` becomes `reqId → {ticketNum, seq}` and `onVerifactuRequestFinished` patches via `updateTicketVerifactuFields(db, ticketNum, result, seq)`. **This changes what is re-submitted to AEAT for `seq>0` events** — intended; it is the fix. The slow-but-already-registered case is still covered by the duplicate-InvoiceID rejection warning. New `test_sql_lite::test_pendingVerifactuEvents` seeds save-time (multi-row), partial-pay (`seq>0`) and ENVIADA/before-floor rows and asserts the grouping, per-event sums and ordering. Files: `src/sql_lite/sql_lite.{h,cpp}`, `src/app/pendingsubmitsdialog.{h,cpp}`, `src/app/mainwindow.{h,cpp}`, `tests/test_sql_lite.cpp`, docs.

### Post-9.1 development — June 2026
- [x] **PayDialog "Cobrar" now auto-prints a single factura**: `PayDialog::printPartialFactura` printed two copies (customer + business: `createTicketExcel(true)` → `printTicket()` → `createTicketExcel(false)` → `printTicket()`). At the operator's request it now prints only the single customer copy after Cobrar; a second copy is available via the RecogPrendas reprint button (`pb_print`). Files: `src/recog_prendas/pay_dialog.cpp`, `docs/modules/recog_prendas.md`.
- [x] **`ReportHtml::formatEuro` now groups thousands consistently**: the test_reporthtml suite had surfaced that `QLocale(Spanish,Spain).toString(value,'f',2)` grouped inconsistently — no separator for `1234.56` (`"1234,56"`) but a single misplaced one for `1000000.0` (`"1000.000,00"`). Replaced the QLocale call with manual grouping: round to cents, group the integer part in threes with `.`, join with `,` and two zero-padded decimals, handling the sign. Now `1234.56 → "1.234,56 €"`, `1000000 → "1.000.000,00 €"`, `999.99 → "999,99 €"`, `-1234.5 → "-1.234,50 €"`. The test was updated to assert the grouped output. Affects every euro figure in the Contabilidad + Listados PDFs (a display improvement). Files: `src/reporthtml/reporthtml.h/.cpp`, `tests/test_reporthtml.cpp`, docs.
- [x] **Test coverage: MainWindow `removeSpecialChar` extracted + tested**: the last quick pure-helper win on the MainWindow/RecogPrendas surface.
